\subsection{Model with Slope
Explanation:}\label{model-with-slope-explanation}

We are modeling pear size over time, accounting for:

Fixed effects: time, climate, species

Random effects: variability among individual pears (pear\_id),
optionally allowing each pear to grow at its own rate (random slope)

Explanation: Allows each pear to have its own starting size (intercept)
and growth rate (slope over time).

More flexible, captures individual growth trajectories.

Warning: boundary (singular) fit: This means the model might be too
complex --- the variance of the slope might be close to zero or highly
correlated with the intercept (as we see here: correlation = -1.00). * A
singular fit occurs when one or more random effects are estimated to
have: * Variance ≈ 0 (i.e., not contributing anything to the model), or
* Perfect or near-perfect correlation with another random effect (e.g.,
correlation = ±1.00).

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Variance for slope is small (0.01246), possibly unidentifiable.
\item
  Lower REML(Restricted Maximum Likelihood) = 14217.8 means \#\# Model
  Interpretion: Time: Highly significant positive effect → pears grow
  \textasciitilde0.51 cm/week.
\end{itemize}

Climate Scenarios: Scenario 2 and 3 significantly reduce growth compared
to Scenario 1.

Species: Doyenne pears are \textasciitilde0.89 cm smaller than baseline
(Conference).

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# 4. Fit Linear Mixed Models}

\DocumentationTok{\#\# 4a. Random intercept model (basic model)}
\NormalTok{model1 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{lmer}\NormalTok{(size }\SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}}\NormalTok{ time }\SpecialCharTok{+}\NormalTok{ climate }\SpecialCharTok{+}\NormalTok{ species }\SpecialCharTok{+}\NormalTok{ (time }\SpecialCharTok{|}\NormalTok{ pear\_id), }\AttributeTok{data =}\NormalTok{ size\_data)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\end{verbatim}

\begin{Highlighting}[]

\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

## lmerModLmerTest]

##    Data: size_data
## 
## REML criterion at convergence: 14217.8
## 
## Scaled residuals: 
##     Min      1Q  Median      3Q     Max 
## -3.6388 -0.5893 -0.0183  0.5169  5.7521 
## 
## Random effects:
##  Groups   Name        Variance Std.Dev. Corr 
##  pear_id  (Intercept) 0.34210  0.5849        
##           time        0.01246  0.1116   -1.00
##  Residual             1.10163  1.0496        
## Number of obs: 4625, groups:  pear_id, 185
## 
## Fixed effects:
##                     Estimate Std. Error         df t value Pr(>|t|)    
## (Intercept)        2.030e-01  6.624e-02  5.298e+02   3.064 0.002293 ** 
## time               5.074e-01  8.482e-03  1.843e+02  59.818  < 2e-16 ***
## climateScenario 2 -3.211e-01  5.989e-02  1.290e+03  -5.361 9.78e-08 ***
## climateScenario 3 -2.205e-01  5.769e-02  1.290e+03  -3.822 0.000138 ***
## climateScenario 4 -7.845e-02  5.851e-02  1.290e+03  -1.341 0.180237    
## speciesDoyenne    -8.939e-01  4.182e-02  1.290e+03 -21.376  < 2e-16 ***
## ---
## Signif. codes:  0 '***' 0.001 '**' 0.01 '*' 0.05 '.' 0.1 ' ' 1
## 
## Correlation of Fixed Effects:
##             (Intr) time   clmtS2 clmtS3 clmtS4
## time        -0.726                            
## climatScnr2 -0.423  0.000                     
## climatScnr3 -0.418  0.000  0.484              
## climatScnr4 -0.423  0.000  0.478  0.498       
## speciesDynn -0.303  0.000  0.002 -0.067 -0.030
## optimizer (nloptwrap) convergence code: 0 (OK)
## boundary (singular) fit: see help('isSingular')
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{Simple Model Explanation}\label{simple-model-explanation}

\emph{Allows each pear to have a different starting size only. }Assumes
same growth rate over time for all pears. \#\# Output \emph{Higher REML
= 16099.4 → worse fit than Model 1. }No singular fit warning → more
stable, but less flexible.

\subsection{Model interpretation}\label{model-interpretation}

Similar interpretation, but: \emph{Species Doyenne now has a stronger
negative effect (1.89 cm smaller). }Slightly stronger climate scenario
effects (Scenario 2 and 3 more negative).

\subsection{Conclusion}\label{conclusion}

Model 1 fits better, but the singular fit suggests that the random slope
for time may not be needed or that the model is too complex for your
data.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{model2 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{lmer}\NormalTok{(size }\SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}}\NormalTok{ time }\SpecialCharTok{+}\NormalTok{ climate }\SpecialCharTok{+}\NormalTok{ species }\SpecialCharTok{+}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{|}\NormalTok{ pear\_id), }\AttributeTok{data =}\NormalTok{ size\_data)}
\FunctionTok{summary}\NormalTok{(model2)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{Uncorrelated Model
Explanation}\label{uncorrelated-model-explanation}

\emph{This model allows time to vary by pear, but doesn't estimate the
correlation with the intercept. }It's a simpler and more stable version
of Model 1

\#\#Benefits \emph{No singular fit }Lower REML/AIC *Reasonable variance
estimates

\#\#Model fit:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The REML criterion (14422.1) is higher than the correlated slope model
  (14217.8) but much lower than the simple model (16099.4), indicating
  it's a good compromise.
\item
  No singular fit warning, so the model is statistically sound.
\end{itemize}

\#\#Fixed effects:

\emph{Time is still a strong, significant positive predictor of size.
}The effect of climate scenarios is no longer significant (p
\textgreater{} 0.05), suggesting the differences between them might not
be strong once random variation in growth is modeled properly. *Species
effect remains significant (Doyenne leads to smaller size).

\#\#Random effects:

There's clear variability between trees in both starting size
(intercept) and growth rate over time (slope for time), but they're not
correlated, which avoids the previous overfitting problem.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{model\_uncorrelated }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{lmer}\NormalTok{(size }\SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}}\NormalTok{ time }\SpecialCharTok{+}\NormalTok{ climate }\SpecialCharTok{+}\NormalTok{ species }\SpecialCharTok{+}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{1} \SpecialCharTok{|}\NormalTok{ pear\_id) }\SpecialCharTok{+}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{0} \SpecialCharTok{+}\NormalTok{ time }\SpecialCharTok{|}\NormalTok{ pear\_id), }\AttributeTok{data =}\NormalTok{ size\_data)}
\FunctionTok{summary}\NormalTok{(model\_uncorrelated)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(dplyr)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(ggplot2)}

\CommentTok{\# Calculate mean size per week per climate}
\NormalTok{mean\_size\_by\_group }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ size\_data }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
  \FunctionTok{group\_by}\NormalTok{(time, climate) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
  \FunctionTok{summarise}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{mean\_size =} \FunctionTok{mean}\NormalTok{(size), }\AttributeTok{.groups =} \StringTok{"drop"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Plot manually}
\FunctionTok{ggplot}\NormalTok{(mean\_size\_by\_group, }\FunctionTok{aes}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{x =}\NormalTok{ time, }\AttributeTok{y =}\NormalTok{ mean\_size, }\AttributeTok{color =}\NormalTok{ climate)) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{geom\_line}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{linewidth =} \FloatTok{1.2}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{geom\_point}\NormalTok{() }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{labs}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{title =} \StringTok{"Average Pear Size Over Time by Climate Scenario"}\NormalTok{,}
       \AttributeTok{x =} \StringTok{"Week"}\NormalTok{,}
       \AttributeTok{y =} \StringTok{"Mean Size (cm)"}\NormalTok{,}
       \AttributeTok{color =} \StringTok{"Climate Scenario"}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{theme\_minimal}\NormalTok{()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\includegraphics{size_files/figure-latex/unnamed-chunk-22-1.pdf}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Calculate mean size by time, climate, and species}
\NormalTok{mean\_size\_species }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ size\_data }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
  \FunctionTok{group\_by}\NormalTok{(time, climate, species) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
  \FunctionTok{summarise}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{mean\_size =} \FunctionTok{mean}\NormalTok{(size), }\AttributeTok{.groups =} \StringTok{"drop"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Plot with facets}
\FunctionTok{ggplot}\NormalTok{(mean\_size\_species, }\FunctionTok{aes}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{x =}\NormalTok{ time, }\AttributeTok{y =}\NormalTok{ mean\_size, }\AttributeTok{color =}\NormalTok{ climate)) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{geom\_line}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{linewidth =} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{facet\_wrap}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}}\NormalTok{ species) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{labs}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{title =} \StringTok{"Pear Size Over Time by Climate Scenario and Species"}\NormalTok{,}
       \AttributeTok{x =} \StringTok{"Week"}\NormalTok{, }\AttributeTok{y =} \StringTok{"Mean Size (cm)"}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{theme\_minimal}\NormalTok{()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{heat\_data }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ size\_data }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
  \FunctionTok{group\_by}\NormalTok{(time, climate) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
  \FunctionTok{summarise}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{mean\_size =} \FunctionTok{mean}\NormalTok{(size), }\AttributeTok{.groups =} \StringTok{"drop"}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
  \FunctionTok{dcast}\NormalTok{(time }\SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}}\NormalTok{ climate, }\AttributeTok{value.var =} \StringTok{"mean\_size"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Melt again for ggplot heatmap}
\NormalTok{heat\_data\_melt }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{melt}\NormalTok{(heat\_data, }\AttributeTok{id.vars =} \StringTok{"time"}\NormalTok{)}

\FunctionTok{ggplot}\NormalTok{(heat\_data\_melt, }\FunctionTok{aes}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{x =}\NormalTok{ time, }\AttributeTok{y =}\NormalTok{ variable, }\AttributeTok{fill =}\NormalTok{ value)) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{geom\_tile}\NormalTok{() }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{scale\_fill\_viridis\_c}\NormalTok{() }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{labs}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{title =} \StringTok{"Heatmap of Mean Pear Size"}\NormalTok{,}
       \AttributeTok{x =} \StringTok{"Week"}\NormalTok{, }\AttributeTok{y =} \StringTok{"Climate Scenario"}\NormalTok{, }\AttributeTok{fill =} \StringTok{"Mean Size"}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{theme\_minimal}\NormalTok{()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}
